It doesn't matter if someone's sitting right next to me. \n\nThat's... honestly, that's what scared me enough to actually come in here. Just... hearing it so clearly when I know the channels are muted, and... knowing no one else in the room hears a thing.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices sound like they are coming from specific people you know?\n\nPatient: No. Just... strangers. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear these voices when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: No. Just... just at work. Like I said, it's when it gets quiet in the monitor room. I'll just be sitting there, and... and they come through the speakers. Saying I missed something, or that someone's going to get hurt. Even when everything's muted. That's... that's what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever with someone else in the monitor room when you hear these voices?\n\nPatient: Yeah... sometimes. Like I said before, there are other guards in there with me. But they don't hear it. I'll hear the speakers say my name, or that I missed something, and... the person sitting right next to me doesn't even look up.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever criticize you about things outside of your job?\n\nPatient: No. Just... just about work. Like I said, it's just when I'm in the surveillance room. The speakers will just say... \"You missed it, Ruth,\" or... \"People are going to get hurt because of you.\" It's just about the monitors.\n\nDoctor: Are you doing something specific on the monitors when you hear the voices?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Just... watching them. Like I said, it usually happens during the quiet stretches. I'm just sitting there, looking at the screens, and... then I hear it. Through the speakers. Even when they're muted.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices just as loud or clear when someone else is in the room with you as they are when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Yeah... they are. Just as loud. It doesn't matter if someone's sitting right next to me. \n\nThat's... honestly, that's what scared me enough to actually come in here. Just... hearing it so clearly when I know the channels are muted, and... knowing no one else in the room hears a thing.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices sound like they are coming from specific people you know?\n\nPatient: No. Just... strangers. I don't recognize them. Like I was saying, it's just... through the monitor speakers. Even when they're muted. Saying I missed something, or... people will get hurt. And no one else hears it. That's... that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices stop once you've heard them, or do they keep talking for a while?\n\nPatient: They... they keep going. Usually for a few minutes. I don't really time it. But it's not just one quick sentence. They... linger.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these voices?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Just... bad, I guess. It makes me feel bad.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices affected your ability to do your job or interact with your coworkers?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess. I... I've messed up some paperwork on my shift recently. And I just... I don't really talk to anyone at work anymore. Or my friends. I just can't keep up. When I get home, I just... do the bare minimum. I don't cook or have people over or anything like that anymore. I just... want to be left alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard the voices every time you've been in the monitor room when it's quiet, or are there times when you don't hear them?\n\nPatient: It's... mostly just during the quiet stretches. That's... that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices stop once you've heard them, or do they keep talking for a while?\n\nPatient: They... they keep going. Usually for a few minutes. I don't really time it. But it's not just one quick sentence. They... linger.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these voices?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Just... bad, I guess. It makes me feel bad.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices affected your ability to do your job or interact with your coworkers?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess. I... I've messed up some paperwork on my shift recently. And I just... I don't really talk to anyone at work anymore. Or my friends. I just can't keep up. When I get home, I just... do the bare minimum. I don't cook or have people over or anything like that anymore. I just... want to be left alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard the voices every time you've been in the monitor room when it's quiet, or are there times when you don't hear them?\n\nPatient: It's... mostly just during the quiet stretches. In the surveillance room. I hear them coming through the monitor speakers, even when I know every channel is muted. And... no one else hears anything. \n\nThat's actually what scared me enough to finally come in. Just... hearing them say things like... you missed it, Ruth. Or that people are going to get hurt because of me.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices been happening more often or staying about the same since they first started?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. I haven't been keeping track. It just happens when it's quiet. I'm not sure if it's more often now or... or just the same.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. The speakers will just say... \"You missed it, Ruth,\" or... \"People are going to get hurt because of you.\" It's just about the monitors.\n\nDoctor: Are you doing something specific on the monitors when you hear the voices?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Just... watching them. Like I said, it usually happens during the quiet stretches. I'm just sitting there, looking at the screens, and... then I hear it. Through the speakers. Even when they're muted.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices just as loud or clear when someone else is in the room with you as they are when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Yeah... they are. Just as loud. It doesn't matter if someone's sitting right next to me. \n\nThat's... honestly, that's what scared me enough to actually come in here. Just... hearing it so clearly when I know the channels are muted, and... knowing no one else in the room hears a thing.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices sound like they are coming from specific people you know?\n\nPatient: No. Just... strangers. I don't recognize them. Like I was saying, it's just... through the monitor speakers. Even when they're muted. Saying I missed something, or... people will get hurt. And no one else hears it. That's... that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices stop once you've heard them, or do they keep talking for a while?\n\nPatient: They... they keep going. Usually for a few minutes. I don't really time it. But it's not just one quick sentence. They... linger.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these voices?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Just... bad, I guess. It makes me feel bad.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices affected your ability to do your job or interact with your coworkers?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess. I... I've messed up some paperwork on my shift recently. And I just... I don't really talk to anyone at work anymore. Or my friends. I just can't keep up. When I get home, I just... do the bare minimum. I don't cook or have people over or anything like that anymore. I just... want to be left alone.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard the voices every time you've been in the monitor room when it's quiet, or are there times when you don't hear them?\n\nPatient: It's... mostly just during the quiet stretches. In the surveillance room. I hear them coming through the monitor speakers, even when I know every channel is muted. And... no one else hears anything. \n\nThat's actually what scared me enough to finally come in. Just... hearing them say things like... you missed it, Ruth. Or that people are going to get hurt because of me.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices been happening more often or staying about the same since they first started?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. I haven't been keeping track. It just happens when it's quiet. I'm not sure if it's more often now or... or just the same.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships or daily activities outside of work since the voices started?\n\nPatient: I mean... I think I said earlier, I don't really have people over anymore. We used to play board games. And I used to go to salsa class, or play softball... I liked building PCs, too. But... I don't know. Lately I just ignore everyone's texts. I just sit at home... like none of it can reach me.\n\nDoctor: Has anything significant or stressful happened in your personal life recently, outside of these voices at work?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. Just... work, and then home. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that someone is out to get you or that there's a conspiracy against you?\n\nPatient: No. I don't think so. I just... I don't really think about that kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a persistent sadness or loss of interest in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I guess so. It's just... most days, there's this heavy, gray kind of mood. It settles over me before I even get out of bed. And... sometimes I'll just be sitting in my car before my shift, and I start tearing up. Just from seeing the casino lights. I don't really know why.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like doing, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: I don't really... do much anymore. I used to go to a salsa class, play softball... I liked building PCs and having friends over for board games. But lately, I just... I ignore their texts. I just sit at home. It feels like... like none of it can even reach me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.     "functional_impairment": true,
```json
    "traumatic_stressor": null,
    "psychosocial_stressor": null,
    "additional_requirements": ["onset of symptoms not clearly linked to a specific traumatic or psychosocial stressor", "symptoms are not better explained by another mental disorder"]
  }
}
```
